The consumer (next conv) reads it as uint8 and dequantizes using its own (sx,zx). If (sx,zx) ≠ (so,zo) of the producer, then the consumer's view of the values differs by a scale factor → wrong. Key considerations for the contract:

§1 formula:
```
acc[c] = Σ_{ci} Σ_{kh,kw} x_q[ci][h*stride+kh-pad][w*stride+kw-pad] * w_q[c][ci][kh][kw] + bias_i32[c]
q_pre  = clamp( round( acc[c] * M[c] * 2^-S[c] ) + zp_pre , 0, 255 )
q_out  = LUT[ q_pre ]
```

§3.2:
```
bias_i32[c] = round( b_f[c] / (sx*sw[c]) ) - zx * Σ w_q[c]
```

§3.3:
```
M[c] = round( sx * sw[c] / sp * 2^31 )
S[c] = 31 + adjustment so that M[c] ∈ [2^30, 2^31)
```
Note: this is circular/inconsistent as written. The standard approach: compute real multiplier = sx*sw/sp. Then find shift n such that M = round(mult * 2^n) falls in [2^30, 2^31). So `n` is the shift count, i.e. `M * 2^-n ≈ mult`. So S[c] = n, not "31 + adjustment". Let me think.

Contract says: `M[c] = round( sx*sw[c]/sp * 2^31 )` (int32, Q31 fixed point), and `S[c] = 31 + <adjustment to make M fall in [2^30,2^31)>`.

If you compute M = round(mult * 2^31), M may not fall in [2^30, 2^31) — M could be e.g. 2^31 * 0.001 = 2.1e6. Then to bring M into [2^30,2^31), you need to multiply by ~512 = 2^9, so the adjustment is +9, and then S = 31 - 9 = 22? Hmm. Let's define: real = acc * mult, where mult = sx*sw/sp. We want acc * M * 2^-S = acc*mult. So M * 2^-S = mult ⇒ M = mult * 2^S. To make M in [2^30, 2^31), S = 31 + ceil(log2(mult))... Actually S must satisfy 2^30 ≤ mult*2^S < 2^31, i.e. S in [30 - log2(mult), 31 - log2(mult)).

The contract's formula says M = round(mult * 2^31) with S = 31 + adjustment. That's equivalent to M = round(mult*2^(S) ) where S = 31+adj if M = round(mult*2^31)*2^adj... no, multiplying M by 2^adj and keeping S=31-adj... Hmm, the contract statement is confusing/wrong. Actually a cleaner reading: S[c] = 31 + adj, M[c] = round(mult * 2^(31+adj))?? No, then M falls in [2^30,2^31) for the right adj, and M*2^-S = mult*... yes! If M = round(mult * 2^S) and S = 31 + adj, then M ≈ mult*2^S ∈ [2^30, 2^31) determines adj. So M[c] = round(mult * 2^S[c]) where S[c] = 31+adj chosen so M in [2^30,2^31). But contract says M = round(mult * 2^31) and S = 31 + adjustment. That reading would break the requant. So contract §3.3 is indeed ambiguous/potentially wrong in wording — this is one of the things I should report. Actually more precisely: the sentence "M[c] = round( sx * sw[c] / sp * 2^31 )（int32，Q31 定点）; S[c] = 31 + <使 M[c] 落在 [2^30, 2^31) 的调整量>" — the only self-consistent reading: define exponent e[c] such that mult*2^e ∈ [2^30, 2^31); then M = round(mult*2^e), S = e. Writing "S = 31 + adjustment" where adjustment = e - 31. Working it out: for mult ≈ 1e-3, e ≈ 41, adj = +10. So M in [2^30,2^31), S=41. Then acc*M*2^-41 ≈ acc*mult. That's self-consistent if we read M = round(mult * 2^S) — but §3.3 literally writes 2^31 in the M formula. So the formula as literally written is wrong unless adjustment is applied to M as well. I need to note this in README and report to Lead, and implement the self-consistent version (M = round(mult·2^S) with M in [2^30,2^31)). Also note the contract requires the rounding: "round( acc * M * 2^-S )". Hardware implements rounding via adding half then shift — I must specify rounding mode exactly: round-half-away-from-zero? round-half-up? Contract just says `round`. That's ambiguity #2 — critical for bit-exactness. I'll define in the reference implementation `round_half_away_from_zero`? Hmm. But for csim comparison, the Lead will implement something. I should implement a specific, documented choice and flag it. Common HLS: `(acc*M + (1<<(S-1))) >> S` — arithmetic shift with add-half. This is round-half-up (ties toward +inf) but note: for negative values, arithmetic shift floors, so add-half gives round-half-up (toward +∞) rather than away from zero. Alternatively `int64 p = acc*M; int32 r = (p + (1<<(S-1))) >> S;`. I'll implement exactly that and document it: "round-half-up (ties toward +infinity), implemented as arithmetic shift right of (p + 2^(S-1))". Wait, careful: Arithmetic right shift on negative rounds toward -inf. So (p + half) >> S = floor((p+half)/2^S) = round-half-up for all p. Yes, exactly round-half-up (ties toward +∞). For p exactly at .5 → rounds up (toward +inf). Good, deterministic and easy in HLS.

Also need to double check negative acc: M is positive (mult positive), acc can be negative. Fine.

§3.4 LUT generation: 
```
x_f = (q - zp_pre) * sp
y_f = act(x_f)
q_o = round(y_f / so) + zo
LUT[q] = clamp(q_o, 0, 255)
```
Rounding here: also need exactness. numpy np.round is banker's rounding (half to even) which differs from C `round()` (half away from zero). For LUT generation, ties are rare but possible. Deterministic is enough as long as documented. I'll use `np.floor(x+0.5)` for positive... but y_f/so could be negative. Round-half-away-from-zero = sign(x)*floor(|x|+0.5). I'll implement that and document as recommended PS convention, noting contract says `round` without specifying.

The plan:
- sx, zx: per-tensor activation scale/zero for the layer input. Derive from golden stats of the input tensor to that conv: max-abs symmetric? The task says "scale 一律从 golden 实测量（max-abs 对称/最小最大非对称）". For QUInt8 with zero_point, asymmetric min-max is standard: sx = (max-min)/255, zx = round(-min/sx) clamped.

But after the Mul, output range is [-0.2785, ...]. Data from golden will tell. If min >= 0, min-max asymmetric would give zx=0 and scale=max/255. That's good.

However there's a critical issue: **the network is quantized layer by layer**. The true end-to-end behavior would be that the conv input is the quantized output of the previous layer (with accumulated error). Here the task only asks per-layer verification against golden (each layer's input is quantized from the *float* golden input tensor), which is the standard per-layer csim check. Good — that's the judgment criterion for hardware: hardware gets uint8 input, produces uint8, and we compare against reference which gets the same uint8 input. The golden comparison is the sanity that the quantization scheme is sane.

So ref_int8_conv.py should:
1. For each layer: read golden input tensor (float16), compute sx,zx from it (per-tensor asymmetric min-max), quantize → uint8.
2. Read golden conv output tensor (float), compute sp/zp_pre from the pre-activation (conv output tensor), and so/zo from the post-activation output (the Mul output tensor).
3. Run fixed-point conv with int32 accumulate + folded bias, M/S, LUT → uint8 out.
4. Compare with quantized golden post-activation: q_ref = clamp(round(y_golden/so)+zo). Report exact-match count, ±1 count, plus stats.

But it does mean the quantization error is measured relative to the best possible scale. That's OK. I should document the limitation.

Note important subtlety for sp/zp_pre: pre-activation tensor per-tensor asymmetric min-max. Then y_f = act(x_f) where x_f = (q-zp_pre)*sp. The LUT maps to q_out. Then so/zo from the post-act tensor. Since SiLU is monotone (for x > -1.278), the mapping q→q_o is monotone so LUT is fine.

Since so/zo are derived from golden post-act, quantizing golden with them gives near-perfect representation (max quantization error half LSB). The reference's value differs due to (a) input quantization error, (b) requant rounding. That's the expected small difference.

Hmm, but there's a subtle big issue: the input scale sx is derived from the golden input tensor of this layer (full float). At runtime on hardware, the PS will feed activations quantized by the *previous layer's* output scale so/zo. In the per-layer check we use a per-layer-optimal sx, which is exactly what the exporter writes to the params. The PS needs to use the same sx when feeding that layer — and that means the PS must NOT re-quantize using previous layer's so but use the layer's sx... conflict!

Wait. This is a real contract issue: For a chain conv→conv, the output of layer i is uint8 with scale so_i/zo_i, and layer i+1's input must be in its own sx/zx. If sx_{i+1} ≠ so_i, PS must requantize between layers (extra work) OR we should define sx_{i+1} = so_i, zo... Actually this is the classic "activation scale sharing": quantized networks usually have one scale per tensor, and a tensor is both the output of layer i and the input of layer i+1. So so_i must equal sx_{i+1}. In YOLOv8 with SiLU, layer i's output goes through multiple consumers (concat), so each conv input tensor's scale just needs to be consistent with whatever produced it, i.e. so of producer = sx of consumer. Since sx is derived from the *same tensor* as so of the producer (the golden tensor value is identical — the input tensor of conv i+1 IS the output tensor of the previous op), deriving per-tensor scale from the same tensor gives the same number *if the same derivation rule is used*. So as long as I derive scale per tensor name (canonical), so_i (for producer layer) = sx_j (for consumer) automatically when the tensor is the same tensor. 

But careful: for producer layer, "so" is derived from the golden post-act tensor name = producer's Mul output = e.g. `_model.2_cv2_act_Mul_output_0`; consumer `/model.2/m.0/cv1/conv/Conv`'s input is `/model.2/Split_output_0`... wait no. Let me check: model.2 cv2 output goes to Split, then Split outputs feed m.0/cv1 and maybe others. Hmm, actually for C2f, `cv2` output goes into Split which splits into 2 chunks; one chunk goes to m.0, one to concat. So the tensor at the conv input is `Split_output_0` which is a *view* of the Mul output — same values as the first half. Scale derived from the split sub-tensor would differ slightly from the scale derived from the full tensor! This is exactly the "per-tensor scale must be consistent across tensors" problem.

Cleanest solution: derive the scale for each *tensor key* from its own golden values (canonical rule: `scale(tensor) = f(vals)`). Then so for the producer layer means the scale of the producer's final output tensor; but if the consumer reads a Split view, the numbers differ. The pragmatic, contract-consistent approach: define scale per tensor (per name), documented as `so = scale(out_tensor_name)`, `sx = scale(in_tensor_name)`, and PS must requantize if they differ (or better: PS should use a shared "tensor scale" per physical buffer).

But actually, in the hardware flow, PS controls the buffers. If layer i produces uint8 buffer B with scale so_i, then layer j reads B with scale sx_j. If sx_j ≠ so_i, there's a mismatch — the params encode sx_j, and the activation values in B were quantized with so_i. Then hardware computes with wrong sx assumption. So the exporter must guarantee sx_j = so_i for chained layers, otherwise the whole pipeline is inconsistent.

Options:
(a) Enforce a canonical global scale per *tensor* using a rule that is view-invariant: e.g. derive from the ONNX tensor name of the producer chain root. Complex.
(b) Derive all scales from a single calibration pass that assigns one (scale, zp) per *quantized tensor name*, and then for the conv input, use the scale of whatever tensor feeds it (its own key). For Split views, instead of deriving from the split output, derive the scale from the pre-split tensor and propagate. That is: build a map tensor→(s,z) where Split outputs inherit the parent's (s,z), Reshape outputs inherit, Identity inherit. That's actually the right thing to do for a real quantizer: quantization params live on tensors; Split is value-preserving so it must inherit.

I think the right engineering answer: compute (s,z) per tensor key from golden, but **propagate through value-preserving ops** (Split, Reshape, Transpose? no—transpose reorders, values same; Concat merges different tensors, can't inherit; Resize interpolates; Add/Concat/MaxPool produce new tensors → derive fresh). Hmm, but for MaxPool/Add/Concat the values change range, and their consumer conv's sx derived from them is fine — but then the "producer" of that tensor is a PS-side op, and the PS must quantize it with sx of the consumer, which is fine since PS does that op itself. The problem only arises when the tensor is produced *by the hardware conv*: then the hardware's so must equal the consumer's sx.

So chain check: for each conv output tensor, find its downstream conv consumers (through value-preserving ops). The constraint is: so(conv) == sx(downstream conv) when connected without intermediate range-changing ops.

Simplest consistent approach that I think is best and defensible: **assign one scale per tensor, computed from golden, with a canonical inheritance rule for view ops (Reshape/Split inherit parent; Identity/Constant nope)**, then check the constraint `so[conv] == sx[consumer conv]` and report violations explicitly. Let me first *measure* how big the divergence is.

Actually — hold on. Let me reconsider. Maybe simpler and much cleaner: derive scales by **per-tensor asymmetric min-max on the golden values of the canonical tensor**, and for the conv layer, define:
- sx = scale of the conv's input tensor (its golden values)
- so = scale of the conv's output *activation* tensor

Then for the *hardware chain*, PS uses sx from the exporter per layer, and does the requantization between layers if needed (PS has numpy, a per-layer requant is cheap: `q = round((q_prev - zp_prev)*s_prev/s_next) + zp_next`). But that's extra error and extra work. Hmm. But actually the PS *must* do something about this anyway because layers are separated by non-conv ops (SiLU is inside the LUT, so conv+SiLU is one hardware call; then MaxPool/Concat/Add/Resize happen in PS). Only *direct conv→conv* chains exist in some places? Looking at consumers: every conv output goes to Sigmoid+Mul (activation) or Reshape. So there is NO direct conv→conv edge in YOLOv8n! Every conv is followed by its own activation (fused LUT) or a Reshape. And after the activation comes Concat/Split/MaxPool/Add/Resize/Conv. Let me verify: e.g. `/model.2/cv2/conv/Conv` → SiLU → Mul output → Split. And the Split output goes to concat and to m.0/cv1 conv. So the edge from conv's output tensor (Mul output) to the next conv input goes through Split (value-preserving).

So the constraint is: so(producer, derived from Mul output) must equal sx(consumer, derived from Split view). Since Split view is a subset of the Mul output, min-max over the subset differs from min-max over the whole. So we DO have a real inconsistency unless we propagate.

Decision: implement canonical tensor-scale map with explicit inheritance:
- Quantize params live on "canonical tensors". Build a union-find over tensors connected by value-preserving ops (Split→outputs, Reshape→output, Identity→output, and maybe Squeeze/Unsqueeze/Flatten).
- For each equivalence class, derive (scale, zp) from the **largest/source tensor** (the one whose values are the actual full buffer, e.g. Mul output) — hmm, but the union-find representative could be any. We should derive min/max over the *union* of values? No — must be the actual buffer contents. In hardware flow, the buffer for producer is the full Mul output; Split is not a copy but a strided view (split on channel axis, chunk contiguous). So the union of Split outputs = the Mul output values exactly. So min/max over union of class members = min/max over Mul output. 

So: for each equivalence class, gather min/max over all member tensors (they partition the same value set, except Reshape which is a bijection), giving the class's true value range. Then every member tensor shares the same (s,z). That's exactly right and view-invariant. 

Let me check: Split of [1,32,80,80] into 2×[1,16,80,80] contiguous chunks along axis 1 — union = whole tensor. Good. Reshape [1,64,40,40]→[1,16,4,2100]... wait, the reshape for cv2.0.2 output: `/model.22/cv2.0/cv2.0.2/Conv` out [1,64,40,40] → Reshape → [1,16,4,2100]. Bijection, same values. Good.

So implement: build tensor-level (min,max) from golden stats; build union-find over value-preserving edges; class (min,max) = min/max over members; scale/zp per class.

Hmm, but there's a wrinkle: for an activation output (Mul) feeding a Split, the class contains Mul output and Split outputs. The conv's `so` derives from the class of its Mul output. And the consumer conv's `sx` derives from the class of its input (a Split output) — same class → identical scale. 

But also `so` for the *consumer*... fine.

What about tensors whose producer is a PS op like Concat: Concat output feeds a conv. Its class = {Concat_output} (Concat is not value-preserving). Derive scale from Concat output values. PS computes concat then quantizes with that scale. Fine — no constraint violated since PS does both.

What about Add (residual)? Add output feeds a conv (`/model.8/m.0/Add_output_0` feeds m.0/cv1?). Add is a real range-changing op computed in PS in float? Wait — no! In the real system, activations are uint8 between layers. Add would be done as: dequant both uint8 inputs to float, add, requantize to the target scale. That's a PS job. OK, not my problem here, but the scale for the Add output must be well-defined → derived from golden Add output. Fine.

Important: for the exporter, each conv layer needs sx/zx for its input tensor. If the input tensor is produced by hardware conv+SiLU, the PS just uses that uint8 buffer, and we need sx == producer's so. With union-find over Mul→Split, satisfied.

But wait, there's another case: the conv input might be `_model.9_Concat_output_0` — fine. And `_model.10_Resize_output_0` — fine. Are there conv inputs that are directly the *Mul output* without Split? Probably not since every conv output has Sigmoid+Mul follower and then split/concat.

Let me now verify the whole-chain consistency empirically after building. I'll add a **chain consistency check** to the exporter: for every conv, find downstream via value-preserving ops until a conv is reached; assert sx(consumer) == so(producer). Report count. ### Design: tools/export_layers.py

Steps:
1. Load ONNX, shape-infer, build node maps: tensor→producer node, consumers.
2. Load golden json + npz (stats from json: min/max; values from npz for the layers we need? For LUT and bias we don't need golden values, only min/max. For verification we need values. Export only needs min/max → use json (small, in-git). Good: exporter can work from json only (plus npz optional for --verify).
   Actually we do need the input tensor's min/max for layer 0: "images" not in golden → must compute from regenerated input. I'll regenerate the deterministic input (documented seed) in the exporter and compute its min/max; also verify against golden conv0 output if npz present.
3. Build union-find over value-preserving ops (Split, Reshape, Identity, Squeeze/Unsqueeze/Flatten) — restrict to those actually present: Reshape, Split, Transpose? no. Also note `Shape`/`Constant` produce int tensors; ignore.
4. For each class: min = min of members' min, max = max of members' max (from golden json stats; empty tensors skipped).
5. Derive (scale, zp) for a class: asymmetric min-max: 
   - if max == min: scale = 1/255? Handle degenerate (empty/all-zero). Use scale = max(|max|,|min|)/255 with zp=0 if range 0. Hmm. Let me define: 
     `s = (max - min)/255`; if `s <= 0` or not finite → fallback to symmetric `s = max(|max|,|min|)/255`, and if that's 0 → s = 1.0 (and report). Then `zp = round(-min/s)` clipped to [0,255]. Then recompute effective range? Standard ORT asymmetric: scale=(max-min)/255, zp=round(-min/scale) clipped. Note that with zp rounding, the representable range shifts a bit; values above max may clip. Acceptable (documented).
   - Also there's a subtlety: golden is float16-stored, so min/max have float16 quantization. Fine.
6. For each Conv node (in graph order), determine:
   - geometry: C_IN, C_OUT, H_IN, W_IN, K, stride, pad from attributes + inferred shapes.
   - support check against §5: K∈{1,3}, stride∈{1,2}, pad∈{0,1}, group==1, dilation==1, C_IN 3..512, C_OUT 16..256, H_IN/W_IN 10..320, H_OUT/W_OUT as formula. dfl → unsupported (W_IN=2100). Mark unsupported with reason; skip.
   - w int8 from wq npz (`key__w`) with C_OUT×(C_IN*K*K) — already flattened? The npz stores q reshaped [cout, cin*K*K]. Check ordering: quantize_weights does `wf = w.reshape(cout, -1)` on ONNX [cout,cin,kh,kw] → so flat index = ((ci*K)+kh)*K+kw = ci*K*K + kh*K + kw. Matches §2 "K*K 内层按 kh*K+kw 展平". 
   - sw from wq npz scale.
   - b_f from ONNX initializer (float32) or 0 if absent.
   - sx/zx from input class; sp/zp_pre from pre-activation output class (the conv output tensor class); so/zo from post-activation tensor class (Mul output if act=silu, else the conv output class).
   - bias_i32 = round(b_f/(sx*sw)) - zx*sum(w_int8)  [int64 then int32 check range]
   - M,S: mult = sx*sw/sp; find e = ceil? Determine e such that round(mult*2^e) ∈ [2^30, 2^31). Standard: e = 31 - ceil(log2(mult))? Let's do exact integer search: choose e0 = max(0, 31 - floor(log2(mult))) then adjust by scanning e in {e0-1, e0, e0+1} for M in range. Safer: use frexp: m, ex = frexp(mult) → mult = m*2^ex, m ∈[0.5,1). Want M = mult*2^S ∈ [2^30,2^31) → S = 31 - ex. Then M = round(m*2^31) ∈ [2^30, 2^31). Good, and clamp M to 2^31-1 if rounding hits 2^31 (m close to 1). Then adjust: if M == 2^31, S += 1, M = 2^30? Hmm. Let's just compute with integer search and assert.
     Also handle mult <= 0 (sw could be 0 if zero channel? scale set to 1.0 in quantize_weights when amax==0, so sw>0 always). sx>0, sp>0.
   - LUT: for q in 0..255: x_f=(q-zp_pre)*sp; y_f = act(x_f); q_o = round_half_away(y_f/so)+zo; clamp 0..255. uint8.
   - act: from consumer of conv output: if Sigmoid+Mul with the conv output feeding Mul's second input → silu; if ReLU → relu; else none.
     Determine: conv output consumers. If exactly {Sigmoid, Mul} and Mul's inputs are [conv_out, sigmoid_out] (or reverse) → silu. Else if consumer ReLU → relu. Else if consumers are Reshape etc → none.
     Note: for the DFL conv (unsupported anyway) act = none.
7. Pack output: 
   - `data/layers/yolov8n_320_layers.json` — manifest: for each layer index: name (ONNX node name), key (ascii-safe), geometry, scales, act, tensors (in/out golden keys), byte counts, offsets into the big binary blobs.
   - Binary blobs as separate files: to be "directly feedable to hardware", provide per-layer .bin files? The contract says batch params, lut, weights. For HLS csim, the TB needs to load them. Simplest for hardware: one directory with per-layer files: `w_K.bin` (int8), `params.bin` (int32 ×3×C_OUT), `lut.bin` (256 uint8), `in_K.bin` (uint8 activation input, from quantized golden — needed for csim!), `out_K.bin` (expected uint8 output = reference result for bit-exact compare!). That's a lovely deliverable: the csim testbench needs in/out vectors. And "总字节数" reporting.
   
   Hmm, but data/layers with 63 layers × (weights up to 295KB + in/out up to 409KB) ≈ maybe 4MB weights + inputs/outputs. Let's estimate: total weights 3.15MB; inputs: sum over layers of C_IN*H_IN*W_IN — e.g. model.0 3*320*320=307KB; big ones 128*10*10=12.8KB; the biggest input is layer 0 (307KB) and model.2/m.0 16*80*80=102KB, model.4/cv2 128*40*40=204KB... total maybe ~2-3MB. Outputs similar. Total ~10MB. Hmm. That's a lot of files for git. But .gitignore needs to exclude them. Actually the contract task says output `data/layers/...`. I should write the npz + json (json small, npz excluded from git) and only write per-layer .bin files under `--emit-bins` (opt-in, default on? The Lead needs them for csim).

   Let me decide: default output = single npz `data/layers/yolov8n_320_layers.npz` containing all per-layer arrays (w, params, lut, sx.., in_q, out_q), plus the manifest json (small, in git), plus **an optional `--emit-bins`** to write `data/layers/bin/<layer>/w.bin, params.bin, lut.bin, in.bin, out.bin`. And add .gitignore entries for data/layers/*.npz and bin/.

   Wait — I'm told not to touch .gitignore? Write scope is `tools/`, `data/layers/`, `data/results/`. .gitignore is root → outside scope. Hmm. I shouldn't modify .gitignore. So large files may show up in git status... Not fatal, but I should note it to the Lead (ask them to add `data/layers/*.npz` and `data/layers/bin/` to .gitignore). Actually `.gitignore` already has `*.npz` patterns? No, it has specific ones (data/golden/*.npz, data/models/quant/*.npz). So I'll report to Lead that .gitignore needs `data/layers/*.npz` / `data/layers/bin/`.

   Hmm, alternatively store weights only as .bin files under data/layers/ and exclude... still needs gitignore. Also `*.bin` is already in .gitignore globally! Yes: "# --- 大文件 *.bin ---" lists `*.bin`. So writing `.bin` files is automatically git-ignored. And `*.npy` is git-ignored only under data/golden/ and data/results/ and data/models/quant/. So best: write per-layer **.bin** files (auto-ignored) and a small json manifest (in git). Avoid writing a big npz into data/layers (it would be untracked-and-visible). Actually writing an npz is fine as long as it's ignored... `*.npz` globally isn't ignored. Let me check the .gitignore content again: `data/golden/*.npz`, `data/golden/*.npy`, `data/models/quant/*.npz`, `data/models/quant/*.npy`, `data/results/*.npy`, `data/results/*.bin`, `data/results/*.raw`, and global `*.bin`. So `.bin` under data/layers is ignored globally. 

   Decision: **output format = per-layer .bin files under `data/layers/bin/<NNN>_<key>/` + manifest json `data/layers/yolov8n_320_layers.json`**. Also a `.npy`-free design. This gives hardware-ready files and keeps git clean. And for `ref_int8_conv.py`, it can read the .bin files directly (in.bin, w.bin, params.bin, lut.bin) — proving the package is self-sufficient. 

   But wait: the .bin files reference golden npz for in/out vectors. Those are large: total data. Fine, ~10MB local only.

   Also the task says output `data/layers/npz/*.npz`（或单个大 npz）. I'll do .bin + json and explain why (git ignore rules + hardware-friendly raw bytes, no npz header parsing in C++). Actually let me ALSO write a single npz? That doubles disk. Hmm, no: I'll go with .bin + a note in README that npz was rejected because (a) `*.bin` is already git-ignored while `*.npz` isn't under data/layers, (b) HLS TB / PYNQ want raw little-endian bytes with no container. This is a deviation from the suggestion in the task but justified; I'll report it to the Lead. Hmm, risky: Lead may want npz for convenience. Compromise: write both? The exporter can have `--format bin,npz` with default `bin` + optional npz. Overkill. Let me output bins + json, and additionally dump `data/layers/yolov8n_320_layers.npz` when `--npz` is passed. Default off.

   Hmm, actually, for ref_int8_conv.py to be a *bit-exact judge*, it should compute from the package (w/params/lut from .bin) — good.

8. Report numbers: layer count, supported/unsupported, total bytes by category.

### Design: tools/ref_int8_conv.py

- Load package manifest + .bin files.
- For a given layer (or --all): read in.bin (uint8 C_IN×H_IN×W_IN), w.bin (int8 C_OUT×C_IN*K*K), params.bin (int32), lut.bin, run:
  ```
  acc[c][oh][ow] = Σ ... using integer arithmetic, padded with zp_in
  ```
  Do it in pure numpy with int32/int64: 
  - Build padded input: np.full((C_IN, H_IN+2*pad, W_IN+2*pad), zp_in, uint8); assign.
  - For each (kh,kw) in K×K: x_slice = padded[:, kh + stride*arange(H_OUT) ...]. Use strided slicing: `padded[:, kh:kh+stride*H_OUT:stride, kw:kw+stride*W_OUT:stride]` shape (C_IN,H_OUT,W_OUT). Then einsum with w[:, :, kh, kw] as int32 → int32 accumulate. Careful with dtype: use int32 arrays. x as int32 (from uint8), w as int32 from int8. Product ≤ 16129; sum over C_IN*K*K up to 512*9=4608 terms × 16129 ≈ 7.4e7 < 2^31. Contract says max ~2^27 — fine, int32 safe.
  - Then acc += bias_i32 (int32, but intermediate could overflow? bias_i32 magnitude = |b/(sx*sw)| + zx*|Σw| ≤ 255*4608*127 ≈ 1.5e8, so ~1.5e8 + 7.4e7 < 2^31. Use int64 to be safe then cast, and assert within range.)
  - requant: p = acc.astype(int64) * M → up to 1.5e8*2^31 ≈ 3.2e17 < 2^63 fine. Then r = (p + (1 << (S-1))) >> S with arithmetic shift on int64 numpy (numpy >> on signed ints is arithmetic). Then q_pre = clip(r + zp_pre, 0, 255).astype(uint8). Then out = lut[q_pre].
  
  Wait, contract says `q_pre = clamp(round(acc*M*2^-S) + zp_pre, 0, 255)`, then `q_out = LUT[q_pre]`. Note clamp happens *before* LUT. Good, matches.

  Compare with out.bin (which is the exporter's stored reference output) → must be bit-exact identical. And also compare vs golden re-quantized (the "逐位比对" acceptance item 4).

- Also need `--layer NAME` comparison against golden. So the tool needs access to golden npz + the manifest's golden keys + scales. For golden comparison: q_gold = round_half_away(golden_postact/so)+zo clipped. But out.bin already equals that? No: out.bin = ref output. Golden comparison needs floats. So I'll store in the manifest the golden key names, and ref_int8_conv reads golden npz directly. Good — that also double-checks make_golden consistency.

Let me now think about what the "reference output" stored in out.bin is. It should be the reference implementation's result (from the same fixed-point pipeline). But then ref_int8_conv just recomputes and compares to its own stored value — a regression check, plus golden comparison gives the real signal. To be careful, the exporter should compute out.bin *independently* — but sharing code means a bug is shared. Hmm, "硬件 csim 结果必须与你逐元素相同" — the csim TB needs in.bin/out.bin. If both come from the same numpy code, that's a self-consistency trap, but the golden comparison is the external check. I'll implement the conv core in ref_int8_conv.py and have export_layers.py *import* it? No — files must be standalone-runnable. Better: put the core in ref_int8_conv.py and import it from export_layers.py (`import ref_int8_conv as ref`), with `sys.dont_write_bytecode = True` at top of both. Since they're in the same dir, need sys.path handling: `sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))`. That's fine and avoids duplication. Hmm, but "脚本 import 同目录模块，顶部加 sys.dont_write_bytecode=True" — allowed, they anticipated it.

Wait, but if out.bin is generated by the same code ref_int8_conv uses, then `--all` comparing ref vs out.bin is trivially true, and the real check is ref vs golden. Acceptance #4 explicitly wants the golden comparison. So: `--all` prints both: (a) package self-consistency (ref vs stored out.bin: must be 0 diffs), (b) ref vs golden requantized (statistics).

Hmm, actually is storing out.bin even necessary? The csim TB needs expected output. Alternatives: TB could read out.bin generated by the exporter. Yes, keep it — generated by the exporter via the shared core.

But hmm — there's a "shared bug" risk: exporter's LUT generation (float rounding) and ref's requant (int) are separate paths, so at least LUT↔requant interplay is cross-checked. Good enough, plus golden gives external ground truth.

Now, what tolerance for "ref vs golden"? Differences will be ±1 LSB in places due to input quantization. Item 4 says "如果对不上，先把差异特征查清楚". Expect: max |diff| small (1-2), mean tiny, and no systematic bias. I'll report: exact-match fraction, ±1 count, max abs diff, mean signed diff (checks for systematic offset — this detects the -zx*Σw bug!), and per-channel mean bias (detects missed zero-point folding).

Actually, this is a great validity test: if I omit -zx*Σw, per-channel bias would show. I can even add a `--mutate` flag to demonstrate... no, keep it simple but I can mention the diagnostic in README.

One important question: will ref vs golden actually match to ±1? Let's reason: ref computes with input quantized to uint8 by sx/zx. Golden float input quantized then dequantized differs by up to sx/2 = max_range/510. Then conv accumulates this error over C_IN*K*K terms; errors are correlated (quantization error of same value) but roughly random → error in pre-act ≈ sqrt(N)*sx/2*|w|... Then requantized with sp. The relative error vs sp: The output q value error ≈ (input quant error propagated)/(sp). Could be a few LSB. For the first layer maybe bigger. That's the "quantization error" test which is exactly what int8_sim.py measured end-to-end (it passed). We'll see actual numbers.

Let me now also double check: for layer 0, the input tensor "images" range is standard normal, min≈-5.3, max≈5.3. Symmetric-ish. Asymmetric min-max: s=(max-min)/255, z=round(-min/s). Fine.

Another subtlety for sp/zp_pre of act=none layers: the pre-act tensor is the conv output; the post-act tensor is also the conv output (key `_..._Conv_output_0`). Same class → same (s,z). Good.

Now, an important check: does golden contain the pre-activation tensor for every conv? Yes, `_..._conv_Conv_output_0` for all 64. And post-act `_..._act_Mul_output_0` for 57 (63 minus 6 reshape-output layers?). Let me count: convs whose consumer is Sigmoid+Mul = 58? Let's count from output: 64 convs total, 6 go to Reshape: cv2.0.2, cv2.1.2, cv2.2.2, cv3.0.2, cv3.1.2, cv3.2.2 (6) + dfl/conv (1) = 7 → 57 with SiLU. ```

Now let me also double check the `-zx*Σw` sign. Derivation: 
y_f = Σ(ci)(x_q - zx)*sx * w_q*sw + b_f  (note: b_f is the ONNX bias, in float)
Hmm careful: ONNX Conv: y_f = Σ x_f * w_f + b_f where x_f = (x_q - zx)*sx, w_f = w_q*sw.
acc[c] = Σ x_q*w_q + bias_i32[c]
y_f = sx*sw*Σ (x_q - zx) w_q + b_f = sx*sw*(acc_Σ - zx*Σw) + b_f where acc_Σ = Σ x_q w_q.
We want the requant stage to compute y_f/sp:
y_f/sp = (sx*sw/sp)*(acc_Σ - zx*Σw) + b_f/sp
So with acc[c] = acc_Σ + bias_i32[c], we need:
M*2^-S * acc ≈ y_f/sp
i.e. mult*(acc_Σ + bias_i32) = mult*acc_Σ - mult*zx*Σw + b_f/sp
→ mult*bias_i32 = -mult*zx*Σw + b_f/sp
→ bias_i32 = -zx*Σw + (b_f/sp)/mult = -zx*Σw + b_f/(sx*sw). ✓ matches §3.2.

Good. And the reference must compute acc with x_q (uint8, no zero point subtraction) — exactly as contract.

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""ref_int8_conv.py -- bit-exact numpy reference for the PL conv_engine (contract docs/accel_interface.md §1/§2/§3).

... long docstring with the exact fixed-point spec, rounding conventions, and how it is judged.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import hashlib
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

sys.dont_write_bytecode = True

import numpy as np

REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
LAYERS_DIR = REPO / "data" / "layers"
DEFAULT_MANIFEST = LAYERS_DIR / "yolov8n_320_layers.json"
GOLDEN_NPZ = REPO / "data" / "golden" / "yolov8n_320_golden.npz"


def round_half_away_from_zero(x):
    """Round to nearest, ties away from zero == C round() semantics (NOT numpy np.round!)."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    return np.sign(x) * np.floor(np.abs(x) + 0.5)
```
Careful: np.sign(0)*... = 0. floor(0.5)=0 → 0*0=0. ok. For x=0.5 → floor(1.0)=1 → 1. For -0.5 → -1. Good.

```python
    # also match by suffix
```

```python
    g = L["geom"]
    cin, cout, k = g["C_IN"], g["C_OUT"], g["K"]
    w = np.fromfile(root/L["files"]["w"], dtype=np.int8).reshape(cout, cin*k*k)

    outq = np.fromfile(root/L["files"]["out"], dtype=np.uint8).reshape(cout, g["H_OUT"], g["W_OUT"])
    return dict(w=w, bias=params[:,0].astype(np.int64), M=params[:,1].astype(np.int64), S=params[:,2].astype(np.int64), lut=lut, inq=inq, outq=outq)
```

```python
def _conv_engine(in_q, w, bias_i32, M, S, lut, zp_in, zp_pre, stride, pad):
    cin, hin, win = in_q.shape
    cout, cinw, kk = w.shape
    k = int(round(kk ** 0.5))
    w4 = w.reshape(cout, cin, k, k).astype(np.int32)
    hout = (hin + 2*pad - k)//stride + 1
    wout = (win + 2*pad - k)//stride + 1
    xp = np.full((cin, hin+2*pad, win+2*pad), zp_in, dtype=np.uint8)
    if pad: xp[:, pad:pad+hin, pad:pad+win] = in_q
    else: xp[...] = in_q  (avoid copy issue)
    acc = np.zeros((cout,hout,wout), np.int32)
    x32 = xp.astype(np.int32)
    for kh in range(k):
        for kw in range(k):
            xs = x32[:, kh:kh+stride*hout:stride, kw:kw+stride*wout:stride]
            acc += np.tensordot(w4[:,:,kh,kw], xs, axes=([1],[0])).astype(np.int32)
    acc64 = acc.astype(np.int64) + bias_i32[:,None,None]
    ...
```
Hmm acc += tensordot could overflow int32 silently if huge. Use int64 accumulation for the ref? Contract says hardware uses int32. If the true value exceeds int32, hardware would wrap. To be bit-exact with hardware, must model int32 wrap. But if values exceed int32, the design is broken. Better: accumulate in int64, check bounds, and error if |acc| >= 2^31 (report as contract violation). Since math is exact in int32 for our data, no difference. I'll accumulate in int64 and assert range. Also compute max |acc| for reporting.

Actually careful: acc must be int32 in hardware; if |acc| max < 2^31 everything's identical. Report max abs acc. Good.

requant by unique S:
```python
q_pre = np.empty((cout,hout,wout), np.int64)
for s in np.unique(S):
    idx = np.nonzero(S == s)[0]
    p = acc64[idx] * M[idx][:,None,None]
    half = 1 << int(s-1)  # s>=1
    q_pre[idx] = np.floor_divide(p + half, 1 << int(s))
q_pre += zp_pre
q_pre = np.clip(q_pre, 0, 255).astype(np.uint8)
out = lut[q_pre]
```
Note `np.floor_divide` on int64 exact. Assert s>=1.

Also check p range: |acc| ≤ 2^31, M ≤ 2^31 → p ≤ 2^62, +half fine. Good.

Let me write `derive_scale(mn, mx)`:
```python
def derive_quant(mn, mx):
    """min-max asymmetric QUInt8 params, computed from measured float values only."""
    if not np.isfinite(mn) or not np.isfinite(mx): raise
    if mx < mn: raise
    rng = mx - mn
    if rng <= 0:
        # constant tensor: fall back to symmetric magnitude; never invent a nonzero range
        mag = max(abs(mx), abs(mn))
        if mag <= 0: return (1.0, 0)  # degenerate all-zero; scale=1 documented
        s = mag / 127.0; z = 128
    else:
        s = rng / 255.0
        z = int(round(-mn / s))  # ties? use our C-style round
        z = min(255, max(0, z))
    return (float(s), int(z))
```
Use our round_half_away for z too. For all-zero tensor: s=1, z=0 — hmm, would produce q=0 for all values 0 → round(0/1)+0=0. Fine. Are there all-zero tensors? maybe some conv output totally zero? Unlikely. Report if encountered.

def _kernel_size(kk, cin_w, cin):
    if cin_w == 0 or cin == 0: raise
    k = int(round((kk // cin) ** 0.5)); assert cin*k*k == kk

Implementation of conv:

```python
def conv_engine_acc(x_q, w, bias_i32, zp_in, stride, pad):
    """§1 line 1 only: acc[c] = sum(x_q*w_q) + bias_i32[c], int64 accumulate."""
    cin, hin, win = x_q.shape
    cout, cin_w, kk = w.shape
    k = int(round((kk // cin) ** 0.5))
    if cin == 0 or k * k * cin != kk:
        raise SystemExit("weight layout mismatch: cout=%d cin*k*k=%d, got %d" % (cout, cin * k * k, kk))
    hout = (hin + 2 * pad - k) // stride + 1
    wout = (win + 2 * pad - k) // stride + 1
    if pad:
        xp = np.full((cin, hin + 2 * pad, win + 2 * pad), zp_in, dtype=np.uint8)
        xp[:, pad:pad + hin, pad:pad + win] = x_q
    else:
        xp = np.ascontiguousarray(x_q)
    acc = np.zeros((cout, hout, wout), dtype=np.int64)
    w4 = w.reshape(cout, cin, k, k).astype(np.int32)
    x32 = xp.astype(np.int32)
    hspan = stride * hout; wspan = stride * wout
    for kh in range(k):
        for kw in range(k):
            xs = x32[:, kh:kh + hspan:stride, kw:kw + wspan:stride]
            acc += np.tensordot(w4[:, :, kh, kw], xs, axes=([1], [0]))
    acc += bias_i32.reshape(-1, 1, 1)
    return acc, (hout, wout)
```

requant:
```python
def requant(acc, M, S, zp_pre):
    cout = acc.shape[0]
    q = np.empty(acc.shape, dtype=np.int64)
    for s in np.unique(S):
        s = int(s)
        if s < 1: raise SystemExit(...)
        idx = np.nonzero(S == s)[0]
        p = acc[idx] * M[idx].reshape(-1,1,1)
        q[idx] = np.floor_divide(p + (1 << (s-1)), 1 << s)
    q += zp_pre
    np.clip(q, 0, 255, out=q)
    return q.astype(np.uint8)
```
M as int64 array.

Then `out = lut[q_pre]`.

Note: contract's formula is `clamp(round(...) + zp_pre)`. So add zp after rounding. ✓.

For the LUT build in exporter:

```python
def build_lut(act, sp, zp_pre, so, zo):
    q = np.arange(256, dtype=np.float64)
    x = (q - zp_pre) * sp
    if act == "silu":
        y = x / (1.0 + np.exp(-x))     # stable enough for |x| < ~50; use expit-like
    elif act == "relu":
        y = np.maximum(x, 0.0)
    elif act == "none":
        y = x
    else: raise
    qo = ref.round_half_away_from_zero(y / so) + zo
    return np.clip(qo, 0, 255).astype(np.uint8)
```
Note: sigmoid overflow for large negative x: exp(-x) with x=-50 → exp(50)=5e21 fine; x=-700 → overflow warning → inf → y = -700/inf = -0. Fine but warnings. Use np.errstate and clip x to [-100,100]. Pre-act range is bounded by sp*255, typically < 30. I'll clip x to [-200,200] to avoid overflow warnings.

Wait — but for silu: x*sigmoid(x) for x very negative → x*exp(x) → -0. For x = -200: exp(200) = inf-ish (overflow at 709). -200 → exp(200)=7e86 fine. OK clip to [-500,500].

M,S:
```python
def requant_mul_shift(mult):
    # choose S so that M = round(mult*2^S) in [2^30, 2^31)
    m, ex = np.frexp(mult)  # mult = m * 2^ex, 0.5<=m<1
    S = 31 - ex
    M = int(ref.round_half_away_from_zero(m * (1 << 31)))
    if M >= (1 << 31):
        M >>= 1; S += 1   # extremely rare, keeps M in range
    if M < (1 << 30): ... shouldn't happen
    return M, S
```
Check: m ∈ [0.5,1) → M ∈ [2^30, 2^31). If m*2^31 rounds to 2^31 (when m>1-2^-32) then M=2^31 → shift. Then M=2^30, S+=1 — then M in range. Good.

frexp on numpy float64 returns (mantissa, exponent) as arrays; use math.frexp for scalar. Use `math.frexp`.

Key details for the exporter:

- For each Conv node: find its input tensor name `X`, weight init `W`, bias init `B`.
- Get shape of X: from inferred shapes, fallback golden json shape (json has `shape` per tensor). Note golden json `shape` for `images`? No, images not in golden. So first layer use [1,3,320,320] from graph input.
- Determine golden key: `k(name) = name.replace('/','_').replace(':','_')`.
- pre_act key = k(conv_output_name)
- post_act key: check k(conv_output_name.replace('output_0','act_Mul_output_0'))? No — the act node names differ. Better: build the graph's node map to find the actual consumer. Approach: for conv output tensor name `T`, find node whose input T and op_type == 'Sigmoid'. Then that sigmoid's output `S`. Then find node with op_type=='Mul' having S as an input; its output is the post-act tensor. Then post_act key = k(that).
- act type: if found → 'silu'; else 'none'.
- is_graph_output: for reference.

For quant:
- sx, zx: for the conv's input tensor T_in. If T_in == 'images' (graph input): derive from data deterministically → but need to match ORT. Else: from golden json `[k(T_in)]` min/max.
- sw[c]: wq npz.
- sp, zp_pre: from golden json k(T_convout).
- so, zo: from golden json k(T_post) if act else same as sp/zp_pre.

- bias_f: from onnx initializer (float32), length C_OUT. For dfl conv, no bias → skip anyway (unsupported).

- bias_i32[c] = round_half_away(b_f[c] / (sx*sw[c])) - zx * sum(w_q[c])

So acc_f = sx*sw*Σ(x_q-zx)*w_q = sx*sw*(Σ x_q*w_q - zx*Σw_q). Add b_f. We want integer acc ~ acc_f/(sx*sw) = Σx_q*w_q - zx*Σw_q + b_f/(sx*sw). So bias_i32 = round(b_f/(sx*sw)) - zx*Σw_q. Critical design decision: the LUT maps pre-activation quantized value (in the domain of sp/zp_pre) to output quantized value (in domain so/zo). The pre-activation tensor (conv output) has its own scale sp, zp_pre derived from its min/max. The post-activation tensor has scale so, zo.

But in a real quantized network, the conv output's quantization params after requant are... For our hardware, we compute q_pre = requant(acc) + zp_pre, then LUT. So the pre-activation domain needs sp/zp_pre = the quantization params of the conv output tensor as calibrated. And so/zo = params of the post-act (SiLU output) tensor.

Design of manifest:

```json
{
  "schema": "zynq-yolo/layers/v1",
  "generated_by": "tools/export_layers.py",
  "source": {
     "onnx": "data/models/yolov8n_320.onnx",
     "onnx_sha256": "...",
     "weights": "data/models/quant/yolov8n_320_wq.npz",
     "golden_json": "data/golden/yolov8n_320_golden.json",
     "golden_npz": "data/golden/yolov8n_320_golden.npz",
     "contract": "docs/accel_interface.md sec 2/3/5",
     "input_seed": 20261019
  },
  "calibration": {
     "method": "MinMax per-tensor over one deterministic input; scale=(max-min)/255; zero_point=clamp(round(-min/scale),0,255); stats in float64 from data/golden/*_golden.json (float32-exact min/max)",
     "note": "..."
  },
  "totals": {"layers":63, "unsupported":1, "weight_bytes":..., "params_bytes":..., "lut_bytes":..., "in_bytes":..., "out_bytes":..., "all_bytes":...},
  "layers": [ {...} ],
  "unsupported": [ {...} ]
}
```

Each layer:
```json
{
 "idx": 0, "key": "_model.0_conv_Conv", "onnx_name": "/model.0/conv/Conv",
 "geom": {"C_IN":3,"C_OUT":16,"H_IN":320,"W_IN":320,"H_OUT":160,"W_OUT":160,"K":3,"stride":2,"pad":1},
 "quant": {"sx":..,"zx":..,"sp":..,"zp_pre":..,"so":..,"zo":..,"sw":[...],"M":[...],"S":[...]},
 "act": "silu",
 "files": {"w":"bin/_model.0_conv_Conv/w.bin", ...},
 "bytes": {"w":..., "params":..., "lut":..., "in":..., "out":...},
 "golden": {"in":"_model.0_conv_Conv_input_0"?...}
}
```

- export writes `bias_f32.bin` (C_OUT float32, the ONNX bias, zeros if none) and `sw_f32.bin` (C_OUT float32 weight scales).
- ref implements `float_engine()`:

  y = Σ x_f * (w_q*sw) + b_f   — via tensordot on float64
  y_act = act(y)
  q = clamp(round(y_act/so)+zo, 0,255)
  Compare to int32 path output.
This checks §3.2 (bias folding), §3.3 (M/S), §3.4 (LUT) all at once, and enables a per-channel-offset diagnosis.

Expected agreement: differences only from fixed-point rounding of M/S (relative error 2^-31 per channel → negligible) and requant rounding vs true product rounding. Since M/S approximate m to 31 bits, and requant does add-half-floor on int64, the difference vs float rounded value should be 0 or ±1 LSB at ties only. Actually: int path computes floor((acc*M + 2^(S-1))/2^S) which approximates round(acc*m). The approximation error: M = round(m*2^S) has relative error ≤ 2^-31-ish → absolute error in scaled value = acc*m*2^-31 ≤ tiny. So the int result should equal round(acc*m) except exactly at ties. So agreement should be ~100% except tie cases, and never more than 1 LSB.
